ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Prime Minister! Polish society is currently facing challenges arising from changes in the demographic structure of the population. The two main phenomena resulting from demographic change are, on the one hand, low fertility and, on the other hand, an increasing number of older people. Therefore, social policy towards older people is one of the priorities of public policy for the entire Council of Ministers, the United Right Government and Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki. The aim of social policy towards older people is to improve the quality of life of senior citizens by enabling them to remain as independent, active and secure as possible. This security means a sense of freedom from poverty, as well as freedom from anxiety about our own health, mental and physical security in all aspects of the social life of the elderly. Our policy towards older people is to implement measures to guarantee social security for older people, i.e. freedom from the lack of means or conditions to meet the basic needs that will arise in life or after the end of work. Elderly people make a huge contribution to the development of each family by cultivating and developing intergenerational ties. They are the ones who carry out the continuity of generations and share their relationship with one another. For the experience of the elderly is an invaluable treasure, and their counsel, knowledge, and wisdom can always serve younger generations. The Government of Law and Justice appreciates this and proposes favourable solutions for older people who have their professional activities behind them. High Izbo! On 23 February, additional assistance was announced at the Law and Justice Convention for pensioners and pensioners in the form of a 13th pension for each senior. The announcement of Prime Minister Jarosław Kaczyński is implemented by Prime Minister Morawiecki, and our road map is step by step, well implemented. We want to implement it by paying single cash benefits for all recipients. The Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy prepared a draft law on one-off cash provision for pensioners in 2019. The aim of this single benefit in addition to assistance to pensioners is in particular to reduce the income gap in society, including with regard to the income of older people in retirement, pensions or other benefits, e.g. benefits or pre-retirement benefits or social benefits. We propose payment of one-off cash benefits in the amount of the lowest pension, which since 1 March of this year is 1100 PLN gross, net of 888.25 PLN. It responds to the expectations of Poles, responds to the needs of pensioners and pensioners. This is confirmed by the CBOS study, which in March asked Poles about the assessment of our Friday, Fridays of Law and Justice. This is the highest, best score of all regarding the proposals of the Fiveth Law and Justice. "Emerity+" is part of our long-term policy of supporting older people, pensioners and pensioners, as I mentioned at the outset. We're putting a lot of weight on it. This was mentioned in his speech by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, stressing that we have restored the previous retirement age. Much of the participants present in this Chamber have increased their retirement age in spite of what the Poles thought of this decision, contrary to social protests. In 2016, we paid one-off allowances for pensioners who have lower benefits in addition to the statutory valorization. These were varied allowances: the highest supplement was 400 PLN and was due to those who had the lowest benefit. In 2017, our government raised the lowest pension from PLN 882,56 first to PLN 1000, it was an increase of 13 percent, and today we have this benefit of PLN 1100. Last year's valorization has been the highest for 5 years, while in 2019 the valorisation - said the Prime Minister - is a quota-percent benefit and there are no such people who have the required internship and would receive a valorization lower than 70 PLN. And we remember that it was 5 PLN, 6 PLN or not much more. In total, the lowest benefits for pensioners and pensioners increased by 1/4 between 2016 and 2019. In 2015, PLN 3.5 billion was allocated for valorisation; in 2018 it was PLN 5960 million; in 2019 the cost of March valorisation was foreseen for PLN 7900 million. In May we propose payment of one-off benefits - I mentioned this - 1100 PLN gross. All pensioners and pensioners will receive the benefit. As regards the benefits paid by the Social Security Office, the solution will apply in the case of 5740,000. pensions, 1230 000. family pensions, 753 thousand. Rentists who take benefits for incapacity to work. This will be provided to those who have teacher compensation benefits, who receive bridge pensions, who also have paid benefits from the state budget to be paid by the CRUs and the CSO. In the case of the KRUS, this will apply to the pensions of the KRUS system, it is 901 thousand, pensions for incapacity for work - 197 thousand, 43 thousand. Family pensions, I'm talking about the KRUS system itself. They will also receive this benefit from those who collect pensions, uniform pensions, 101 000. people receiving benefits and pre-retirement benefits, 282 000. Social Rentists and 65 thousand. - those who take Mama+'s pension, the parent's supplementary benefit. In total, 9800 000 will benefit from the "Emerity+" programme. people, and for the implementation of this program we will spend PLN 10 800 million. The one-off cash benefit will be paid in an amount less the personal income tax and the health insurance premium. So the typical amount of the benefit will be PLN 880,25. Retirement+ will be given to all pensioners - this is very important - regardless of the amount of the benefit received and I do not mean pensioners who receive higher benefits, but rather those who also receive lower benefits because they did not fulfil, for example. the criteria for the required internship. So this benefit will be paid without determining the income criterion. In the event of a coincidence, only one single cash benefit will be granted to the entitled person. One single cash benefit will also benefit from a survivor's pension to which more than one person is entitled. In our statutory regulation, we propose that a one-off cash benefit be paid out of office without the need to submit applications, together with the provision, on the date of payment in May: 1, 5, 6, 10, 15, 20 and 25 May. [[Applause]] Those are paydays. Then the monthly pension will be an additional benefit. The exception to these payment deadlines refers to the payment in June, because in June benefits and pre-retirement benefits are paid, and if there are also quarterly payments of some benefits from KRUS, then at the beginning of July, but, of course, in those dates where usually pensioners received them, and already earlier it was mentioned that, as the 1st of May is a holiday, there is a free day, so a significant part will receive this benefit in the last days of April. Decisions on one-off benefits will be issued and benefits will be paid by the competent pension authorities and, in the event of a coincidence, the right to benefits which are paid by the two authorities, the decision shall be issued and one-off benefits paid by the Social Insurance Institution. ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u123	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The priority among the government's actions is to seal the tax system and improve VAT collection. The proposed law is another step towards sealing the system of VAT on goods and services and, importantly, minimising the risk of unfair participation of honest VAT taxpayers in the so-called. VAT carousels. What's with the bill? We want to create a single database containing all information on the status of VAT taxpayers in the form of a publicly available list. The list will be an important tool for them. Taxpayers will be able to verify the current status of registration of their counterparties on the selected day, as the register will be kept electronically and will be available 24 hours a day. It'll be updated every day. The register shall contain information on the different criteria for taxable persons registered as VAT taxable persons, e.g. NIP number, company name or surname if someone is active in an individual form. Importantly, there will also be information about so-called numbers. settlement accounts. This will reduce the risk of entanglement of taxpayers in fraudulent practices aimed at extortion of VAT and will have a positive impact on business conditions. The creation of a single database containing all information on the status of VAT payers will facilitate and speed up the search for data on the entities within it by the counterparties concerned. First of all, we will create a single list of VAT taxpayers. Currently, the information on them is contained in two lists that we keep: the list of taxable persons for which the head of the tax office has not registered or has been deleted from the register and the taxable persons whose registration as VAT taxable persons has been restored. They will be merged into a single list, which will then also be extended to entities registered as so-called. VAT taxable persons active and exempt. Secondly, the proposal provides for the extension of the information available in the database to the billing account numbers for taxable persons registered as VAT taxable persons. These are the numbers to be monitored using the STIR system. This list will be updated once a day on business days and will be made available on the BIP website of the Ministry of Finance in such a way that it is easily verified that the entity is included in the list on a selected day not earlier than during the five years preceding the year in which the entity is checked. Access to the list will also be possible via the CEPiK. The proposed law provides for the deletion of the entity's data after 5 years from the date of expiry of the calendar year in which the head of the tax office did not register the entity as a VAT taxable person or removed the entity from the register as a VAT taxable person. The project also provides for the possibility of deleting or correcting data. The proposed law introduces sanctions for payment to accounts other than those reported in the published list, as well as mechanisms for releasing them. The first sanction shall be linked to the loss of the right to credit expenditure to the cost of obtaining revenue in the event of payment on the non-description account. It will also be possible to increase PIT and CIT tax revenues in the case of payment by a third party who has received payment from the buyer to the account of a non-listed supplier. All transactions that exceed a single amount of 15 000 will be subject to this sanction. PLN, i.e. the amount provided for by the provisions on freedom of business. It is possible for the taxable person or the third party making the payment to a non-figuring account to be released from these consequences in the list of notices of that account to the head of the tax office of the issuer of the invoice. The second sanction concerns the liability of a joint and several purchaser in the event of payment of claims on an account other than the account on the list. It is possible to exclude joint and several liability of the purchaser if the taxable person making the payment to an account other than that on the list of notices of that account to the head of the tax office competent for the issuer of the invoice or payment using the split payment mechanism. We expect that the regulations introduced by the proposed law will have the following effects: sealing the system, further reducing the VAT gap, sealing the system in the sense that we will have more complete information about taxpayers who are subject to monitoring under the STIR system, which will improve the effectiveness of the fight against crime and tax fraud involving the use of third party bank accounts in the settlement of commercial transactions, facilitate the exercise of due diligence for buyers of goods and services, reduce the risks associated with economic turnover, including. the risk of joint and several liability for the supplier's tax arrears, and will generally improve the business conditions by increasing business certainty. I would like to point out that the proposed regulations will not have an impact on the reduction in the revenue of the state budget and local government units. More like we're expecting a certain height. On the other hand, expenditure from the budget is planned to purchase the equipment and infrastructure necessary to build the system. The estimated cost of purchasing equipment and licences will be around 550 thousand. zł. It is important that the implementation of a tool to verify the status of VAT taxable persons will not entail the need for taxpayers to adapt their IT systems, but this will result in the need to adapt their systems in the Ministry of Finance and their IT work in the Ministry of Finance. We assume that the draft law implementing the VAT list will enter into force on 1 September 2019, except for provisions which provide for negative consequences, i.e. sanctions. Here we provide for the entry into force of 1 January 2020, which will enable taxpayers to familiarise themselves with the new regulations and prepare for the changes they have made. Finally, I would like to stress that the introduction of a tool in the form of a free-of-charge list of entities for the verification of the counterparty concerned will have an impact on improving the business conditions by increasing the certainty of economic turnover. By introducing the possibility to verify the data of the counterparty, traders are provided with an important tool to avoid buyers of goods and services being involved in fraudulent VAT fraud. This will also help to seal the VAT system. In view of the above, I kindly ask the High House to refer this proposal to further parliamentary work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

The full list of VAT taxpayers will be kept by the Head of National Tax Administration on the website of the Public Information Bulletin of the Ministry of Finance, as well as access to the data contained in the list will also be possible via the Central Register and Information System on Economic Activity. Thanks to this verification, honest taxpayers will be able to avoid fraudulent procedures to extort VAT. I think there's a problem here, because as we're seeing, the Central Register of Invoices isn't here until today. This delay has been almost three years, so there is a problem. Here we know that the information must be up-to-date on a specific day and this will be verified. The proposal also amends the Act of 11 March 2004 on the tax on goods and services, as well as the Act on personal income tax, corporate income tax, the rules on registration and identification of taxable persons and payers and the Tax Order. The proposed amendments will give the opportunity to verify the taxable person's income in case of payment to a different account than that included in the list, to avoid consequences, but subject to notification of that account, of this fact the head of the tax office responsible for the issuer of the invoice. In addition, the project extends the scope of data not covered by tax confidentiality and also allows the taxpayer to be excluded from the applicable joint and several liability, provided that information on the fact of the irregularity is provided to the head of the tax office. My club, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, is in favour of referring to the further work of this project, where, I think, we will talk about details. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u151	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for all the questions you have asked. I'll try to answer all the questions. The first point raised by Mr Paweł Bańkowski concerns why we want to punish honest entrepreneurs, whether it is against EU law, what are the costs. These questions were asked by several of you, so that I will answer together. As to whether we want to punish honest entrepreneurs, of course we do not want to. Neither this system we foresee nor this law change it. We introduce joint and several liability, we introduce a lack of settlement of revenue costs, but at the same time we have foreseen a mechanism that allows us to free ourselves from this responsibility. It is sufficient that the trader reports to the tax office that his counterparty has asked for payment on an account which is not reported, is not on the list, it is not included in this list of VAT payers. This means that he is not jointly liable and has the cost of obtaining income. If it happens that for the second time his contractor will want him to put on that account that isn't on the list, he won't have to repeat his application every time. Once he reported it, he's safe. It's one report that exhausts his duties and keeps him safe. So that I don't think it's possible to say that we want to punish honest entrepreneurs. For every honest entrepreneur, it is clear that this law increases his safety. It increases it to a very important extent, because it gives it certainty that it pays on the relevant account on the list. We are responsible for verifying that this account is the correct account, because we will verify whether this account is reported to the STIR system. The next question was whether this was in line with European Union law. I assure you that this is all in line with the law of the European Union. You came forward asking what the cost was. We've calculated the hard cost of licenses and equipment we need to acquire. That's about $550,000. zł. It's a very small cost. As far as human resources are concerned, the Ministry of Finance and Treasury will not involve additional human resources just to merge these two lists. You have often asked why we are implementing it so late. We do not implement this so late because what we are doing now is a consolidation of the already existing two lists. We create a better list, giving more information, giving more certainty. It's not like we're just doing something right now. In 2017 and 2018, we introduced mechanisms that already allowed for much better checking of counterparties. Mr Janusz Cichoń spoke about the Central Register of Invoices. We de facto have the Central Register of Invoices. We have a JPK that is folded automatically. We have it in our JPK analyzer and we are taking appropriate action on this. We have daily information about bank transfers. This information is much more important than the invoices issued, because in the case of invoices you can do various things, you can cheat as if someone wanted and was dishonest. Money in the bank, on the other hand, is a tough thing. You can't cheat here. You can't make a fake transfer. The second point is that actually the work on the Central Register of Invoices began in 2015. We've seen that there are some notes in the Ministry of Finance that generally show that there's an idea that there's going to be some costs involved, that it's not known whether it's possible to do it or not. These are generally notes on this principle, in quotes: let's put this cup apart. That's how I'd conclude it. These notes are in the Ministry of Finance. We could probably expose them if you'd like. I don't know if we can, but maybe we can. When it comes to questions about whether the system is properly designed, because it is a huge number of data, whether we are prepared for this, as I said, we are in fact strengthening and integrating the two existing registers. They're already in and they're already operating on quite a lot of data. We're prepared for this. In the Ministry of Finance, we have a team set up to implement IT work related to this amendment. This will be ready on September 1, 2019. I've already told you about the budget implications. You also asked, several people asked why we do not anticipate revenue from this system for the state budget. It's no conspiracy that we don't anticipate income. It's a caution in estimating income, because you have to be careful in estimating income. The cost estimation assumes that the costs will be incurred. You have to be very, very careful in estimating your income. This system is de facto a complement to existing systems. It's kind of like a dot over a dot over a dot. He completes the STIR system because there are still payments in this system that are in the accounts, so-called. RORs. We want to motivate entrepreneurs to move to RRs to the extent that they report an account to handle VAT payments, i.e. those that are subject to VAT. Then we increase our perception of these accounts, increase our analytical capabilities. As I said, there will certainly be positive budgetary effects for this reason, but they are extremely difficult to calculate, because what really involves this law is a good, much better than the existing consolidated register of VAT taxpayers with full information about their status. You also asked whether we were analysing the additional costs that entrepreneurs would have to bear, whether they would actually have to spend some money here. No. Firstly, I would like to draw attention to the fact that an ordinary entrepreneur will have a great advantage in obtaining certainty in economic trade. Thanks to the fact that he has better information on call, thanks to typing in the search engine, he will be able to check his contractor much faster than now, not to mention how it was when he wanted to check out his contractor in 2015, 2014 or 2013, because then he could hardly check anything at all. He could only see if the NIP number was listed as the number that refers to the VAT taxpayer. That's all the businessman could have done at the time. So the whole burden of checking whether his contractor was honest was on the entrepreneur at the time. We take this burden off the entrepreneur, we give him better information. We are introducing greater transparency for fair economic turnover. That is why I would very much ask the High House to support this project, because this is a project that introduces great transparency for economic turnover and really increases the safety of ordinary entrepreneurs, such an ordinary, honest, especially small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. There was also a question about the parliamentary offices. I'll admit, I don't quite understand. Maybe I can explain the question of cash. Cash is acceptable, but in business, we prefer bank transfers. If the amount to be paid exceeds 15 000. zł, this should be made in the form of a bank transfer. This is a general rule that already exists, based on the freedom of business law. According to this rule, the transfer is more than 15 thousand. The zloty must be a bank transfer. As for what we are introducing, we are adapting VAT rules to this existing rule, right? So we say that when we make a transfer in business between traders, he has to be made to this account, which is disclosed on the VAT taxable person's list. Otherwise, there are negative consequences. They may not be performing, right? There may be no need for joint and several liability; such risks may be taken. On the other hand, we want additional collateral to ensure that payment of such a large amount outside the banking system does not represent the cost of obtaining revenue. So that I would like to assure you that we have analysed these costs for entrepreneurs. We believe that these costs will not be relevant to them, that there will actually be no costs here. What's more, we think that with the IT systems we're designing, we're going to put in a tool called an API in the IT slang. It's an IT device used to mass-check contractors. So basically, it's going to be possible to do something like that in the accounting system, if I want to make a payment, there's going to be information: be careful, because this payment goes to an account that doesn't exist in the database, because this system is going to ask itself, check our list, if it's a given NIP number, a given company, a name, that's a bank account number. So the entrepreneur really gets more certainty here and it's going to happen much faster, because it's just going to be an automated and secure system. The system itself will inform the entrepreneur that something is wrong, be careful, maybe take additional action, ask. So, to the extent that this project includes amendments to the VAT Act, I answered all the questions. On the other hand, I don't think this is the place until I answer the questions when the government will bring back the 22 percent stake. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u155	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Poselski Club I am pleased to present the position of the club regarding the government draft law amending the act on the tax on goods and services and some other laws, print no. 3301. The aim of the proposed project is to seal the system of value added tax and to minimise the risk of unwitting participation of taxable persons in VAT carousels. To this end, it is planned to amend the VAT Act, as a result of which the existing information contained in the two lists, i.e. the list of taxable persons for whom the head of the tax office has not registered or who have been deleted from the register as VAT taxable persons and the list of taxable persons whose registration as VAT taxable persons has been reinstated shall be included in one combined list, which shall be extended to entities registered as VAT taxable persons, i.e. data relating to the status of active VAT taxable persons and exempt from VAT. The designer's intention to set up a single database containing all information on the status of VAT payers will facilitate and speed up the search for data on the entities included in that list by the counterparties concerned. There is no doubt that the combination of these registers and their appropriate extension will enable taxable persons to verify information about their counterparties. It is expected that this will reduce the risk of entanglement of taxable persons in fraudulent VAT fraud. Therefore, the Modern Posel Club commends these solutions. However, attention should be paid to the deadline for implementing the new regulations. During the consultation phase, it has been stressed several times that the introduction of all the provisions at one time will result in taxpayers without the possibility of knowing the benefits resulting from the introduction of the list of active VAT taxable persons immediately being burdened with the negative consequences resulting from the simultaneous changes in PIT, CIT and Tax Order. It would therefore be useful to postpone the period of entry into force of the Article. 2, Article 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. 5 of the project submitted. The messages and Members of Modern Affairs are counting on this issue to be raised and revised at the stage of the committee's work. We will therefore vote in favour of sending the draft submitted to the committee for further work.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u158	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Marta Kubiak, Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u159	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On the basis of the article. Article 118 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland of 2 April 1997 presents to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in relation to the draft law amending the Act on the Register of Population, print no. 3296. The main purpose of the proposed changes is to optimise the provision of data from the population records to the entities referred to in Article. Article 46 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, i.e. public entities, and Article. Article 46 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 points 1 and 3, i.e. private entities, the Act of 24 September 2010 on population records. In the current legal situation, the provision of personal and address data collected pursuant to Article is made available. The 8th Act of 24 September 2010 on population records takes place by two tracks. The Minister for Home Affairs at central level shall make data from the national PESEL register and local authorities available from the population registers. The issue of the provision by the Minister responsible for Home Affairs and by municipal authorities of data collected in the PESEL register and the population registers remains unchanged. Given the fact that these entities carry out the task of sharing individual data from the PESEL register and the register of residents to the same entities, the project points to a substantive difference in terms of improving the whole process, namely that the PESEL register is a central register, i.e. a national register, which collects data relating to all citizens, while the register of residents is kept according to the local property of the governor, mayor or president of the city. Currently, the capacity to provide individual data from the register of residents is limited to the territorial power of the municipality authority concerned, which means that the application for data from the local register of residents will not be fully implemented if the person sought by the applicant is not currently registered in the area of the target municipality. At that time, the municipality authority concerned, other than the applicant's reply, that the person sought is not included in the register of residents concerned, may only inform the applicant of the possibility of obtaining the data of the person sought from the national register of PESEL, from which the data on behalf of the Minister responsible for Home Affairs shall be made available by the MMSWiA Personalisation Centre. This generates the obligation to re-invent and centralize the entire process, its operation in one place, and this increases the waiting time for data. The proposed project aims to give the authorities of the municipalities the statutory competence to share the unit data from the PESEL register. In principle, this will be a remote operation, suggesting that an application for individual data can be submitted to any municipality body. This is supported by the fact that the municipalities' authorities have already had access to the PESEL register as of 1 March 2015 and are technologically ready to handle the new task. The solutions proposed in the draft are in line with the regulations applicable to data collected in the National Register of Personal Evidence from which data are made available only by municipal authorities and the procedure itself is also of a remote nature. This convergence also confirms the substantive readiness of the municipalities' authorities to implement the new project. Importantly, the implementation of the new regulations does not involve a burden on the work of municipal authorities. The new solutions will minimise the number of applications for data from the PESEL register and the population registers. The number of applications submitted by public bodies is considerably higher than the number of applications submitted to private operators. In addition, appropriate IT solutions in the PESEL register will eliminate the problem of over-applications. In addition, the proposed law introduces an exception to the general principle of de-locating the process of making the data available from the PESEL register. The application in the same case, in accordance with the proposed rules, can only be submitted to one municipality body. This regulation is intended to counter situations where the applicant simultaneously addresses the same application to more than one municipality body. This also applies to the situation in which the applicant intends to submit a further application in the same case at a later date. In this case, the competent authority for implementation will only be the authority of the municipality which considered the previous application. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I recommend the adoption of a bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u161	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The aim of the amendment proposed by the government is to accelerate the sharing of data from the PESEL register and to release the congestion which was created at the Centre for Personalization of Documents of the Ministry of Interior and Administration. While we understand the need to speed up the handling of queries concerning data contained in the PESEL registry, we believe that the solution proposed by the government unfortunately increases the risk of data leaking from the PESEL registry and access to these data by unauthorised entities. The government proposes that the task carried out by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs be transferred to 2.5 thousand today. municipalities. This will be the exclusive competence of the municipalities and therefore all requests for data, both by public entities and by private entities, will simply be addressed to the municipalities and municipalities will be obliged to reply, as the PESEL register is located, independent of the place of residence of the person to whom the request will be addressed. Therefore, there is some doubt. While municipalities are prepared to handle requests from public entities, which often result from such repetitive activities, including the work of municipal social assistance centres, the implementation of these tasks may be more complex in the case of requests from private operators. We have a fundamental doubt as to whether an official - often a small community - will be able to look at matters relating to the population's records, where there is one job, sometimes a one-and-a-half job, when it comes to people who deal with these tasks on a daily basis - will be able to look at a often complicated case with which he refers to him, for example. The recovery firm. We believe that such an official in a small community, who will be asked to make the data available not only from this community, but after all from all over the country, will simply be vulnerable in a confrontation with a large debt collection company. Already today in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, where private applications are handled, often very complex applications, up to 90% proposals require so much work, that, as the Minister said yesterday, these are cases that have expired. That is why we are proposing a solution that we think is a much safer solution. We see the need to unload this blockage that was created and accelerate the query handling process. Indeed, we propose that municipalities carry out tasks related to the provision of PESEL's data on applications from public entities, while as far as private entities are concerned, we would like this task to continue to serve the Centre for Personalisation of Documents of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration. This would lead to a situation in which repeatable cases, which are indeed more - the general estimates show that this is about 300,000. cases per year - would be dealt with at municipal level, while as regards cases which are more complicated and often simply require expertise in assessing whether the applicant has a legal interest in obtaining such data, the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration should, however, be left to handle such requests. We would also like to draw attention to the fact that the originally planned date of entry into force of the Act, i.e. 1 May 2019, is a completely unrealistic term. Fortunately, this deadline was extended during the committee's deliberations. Today, the vacathio legis has been constructed differently. Local governments will have 30 days to prepare for the implementation of this law. On the other hand, we believe that this period of vacathio legis should be even longer in order for local governments to be able to train their employees mainly in handling these more complex applications when it is necessary to assess whether the applicant has a legal interest in obtaining data from the PESEL register. Therefore, another amendment, which my club has prepared, concerns the extension of the vacathio legis and the entry into force of this law 3 months after the date of the announcement. This means that local registers, including data collected therein, are only part of the national central PESEL register. It is noted that after the entry into force of the Act, public entities without a teletransmission decision will no longer be able to request data in an unlimited manner, since from that date each public entity will be able to obtain up to 300 individual data. After using the limit, a public entity in order to obtain further data will have to request the Ministry of Digitality to issue a teletransmission decision or wait until 1 January of the following year. The proposed law introduces an exception to the fact that the application in the same case can only be submitted to one municipality body, which will counteract the chaos and disorganization of the work of municipal authorities. In summary, the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, taking into account the impact assessment, recommends adopting the solutions contained in the report of the Committee on Printing No 33333, especially since no notifications of interested parties were made or the risks resulting from the proposed regulation were identified.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u191	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, I would like to report on the work of the committee on Parliamentary Printing No 3261. It is a draft law on the submission of certain official documents in the Member States of the European Union. Ladies and gentlemen, in short, this is an implementation that concerns EU law. The proposed Act on the submission of certain authentic documents in the Member States of the European Union serves to apply the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union 2016/1191 on the promotion of the free movement of citizens by simplifying the requirements for the submission of certain authentic documents in the European Union and amending Regulation No 1024/2012 of the European Union. The Regulation, which entered into force on 16 February 2019, has general scope, shall be binding in its entirety and directly applicable in the Member States of the European Union. The main objective of the Regulation, which is to simplify procedures for mutual recognition of documents by the authorities, is, inter alia, pursued. by abolishing the verification requirement for certain official documents issued in one Member State of the European Union to be presented in another Member State. The regulation includes documents relating to birth, life confirmation, death, surname, marriage - this applies to the ability to enter into marriage and marital status - divorce, separation or annulment of marriage, registered partnership - fortunately it does not apply to us - the dissolution, separation or cancellation of a partnership - also fortunately it does not apply to us - the origin of children, adoption, as well as the place of residence or residence, citizenship, lack of registration in criminal records, the possibility of exercising active and passive voting rights in local elections. This Regulation does not impose an obligation on Member States to recognise documents which are not in accordance with the legal order of the State concerned or to issue documents not provided for in the legislation of the State concerned - e.g. in the case of Poland, as I have already said here, this does not apply, for example, to partnerships. The proposed regulation does not contain technical provisions and is therefore not notified in accordance with the procedure laid down in the Regulation of the Council of Ministers of 23 December 2002 on the functioning of the national system of notification of standards and legal acts. The proposed regulation does not affect the activities of micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises. The draft law was published in the Public Information Bulletin of the Government Legislative Centre. As far as the committee meeting is concerned, legislative and editorial amendments were tabled during the meeting, there were 10 of them. They were adopted in unanimous mode, not controversial. The amendment was also accepted, actually an auto amendment, to the article. 6. It clarifies the issues concerning the issue of a multilingual form - a supplement to the official language of the European Union is required, in which, in addition to the Polish language, a multilingual form is to be drawn up. Ladies and gentlemen, the committee has adopted the proposed bill in the course of its work. I therefore submit it to the High House as a draft law to be adopted. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u217	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Minister! We proceed with the procedure of a law of the rank of a law aimed at the enforcement of European Union law. The proposed law serves to apply the EU Parliament and Council Regulation 2016/1191 on promoting the free movement of citizens by simplifying the requirements for the submission of certain official documents in the European Union. The primary objective of this Regulation is to simplify procedures for mutual recognition of documents by the authorities and to abolish the requirement of legalisation for certain official documents issued in one Member State of the European Union. This Regulation covers, inter alia, the scope of this regulation. documents of birth, death, surname, divorce, origin of the child, place of residence or place of residence or finally nationality. Some of the data indicated in Regulation 2016/1191 do not oblige all Member States to recognise documents which are not in accordance with the legal order of the country concerned or to issue documents not provided for in its legislation, e.g. with regard to the Republic of Poland, this will apply to documents dealing with partnerships, because our legislation of this institution does not provide. By abolishing the requirement for legalisation, this Regulation also provided Member States with a tool to verify the authenticity of the documents submitted. This tool is specified in IMI. Within the framework of the verification in this system, the relevant authority will be able to consult the model documents issued by other Member States or to clarify the doubts raised by contact with the authority of another Member State of the European Union which issued the document submitted. The above procedure gives the Polish authorities the opportunity to verify the documents submitted to our country, but also requires that they answer the questions of the authorities of other countries with regard to the documents issued by the Polish authority concerned. Communication in this area will be carried out by means of pre-translated questions and answers. The main task of the central authorities, i.e. individual ministers will coordinate the implementation of the tasks related to the reception and transmission of proposals in IMI. At this point, it is acceptable to raise the fact and to pay particular attention to the fact that the authority responsible for replying to the request for information will be any authority which has issued a questionable document, which means, for example, that in the case of a copy of the act of civil status will be the head of the civil status office, and in the case of a court ruling there will be a court, whereas in the case of decisions concerning nationality, the competent authorities of the MMA or the voivode respectively. High Seym! By proceeding with such important matter, it is worth to articulate from this place that today we are dealing with the issue of official documents, which are not merely documents of name or presumption, but, as we should pay particular attention to, documents appearing in individual laws, and many of them have the rank of codified laws. The issuing of certificates, for example, on the expression of the will to vote or stand as a candidate in elections to the European Parliament or in local elections in a Member State of the European Union is crucial, for example, in relation to the place of residence of the citizen concerned. ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u219	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a parliamentary bill amending the law on the extraction of certain fossils, print no. 3312. The project concerns a 15% reduction the dimension of the tax on the extraction of certain fossils. Currently the biggest taxpayer in copper mining is KGHM Polska Miedź SA, which together with the capital group employs over 33 thousand. employees. The company generates revenue of about PLN 20 billion per year. It is a key economic operator not only in the region, but also in Poland as a whole. KGHM is a global company due to the location of its plants, mines in Poland, Canada, the United States and Chile, as well as its markets. Up to 80% domestic production of KGHM Polska Miedź SA is exported. KGHM is faced with the consequences of investment decisions taken between 2011 and 2015 resulting in an increasing financial burden instead of positive returns on invested capital. At the same time, the company is under pressure of increasing investment costs and needs, in particular the mining part of the business. An important negative impact on the company's situation is the tax on the extraction of certain minerals, the strong construction and the amount of which limit the company's investment opportunities, endangers the ability to maintain the production volume necessary for stable operation. At the end of 2017, the company's debt amounted to over PLN 7 billion. At the end of 2011, i.e. immediately before the introduction of the tax on certain fossils and making key investment decisions, the company reported over PLN 12 billion of free cash. Between 2012 and 2017, the company allocated over PLN 14 billion for the purchase of Quadra FNX and financing of foreign projects, mainly the construction of the Sierra Gorda mine. At the same time KGHM paid PLN 9.5 billion of fossil tax, in 2017 alone it was about PLN 1800 million, and in total more than PLN 24 billion of taxes and charges. Great foreign investments, which have not so far brought the expected return, have resulted in the suspension of important domestic investments, on which the future of the Polish part of KGHM depends. With the current level of debt and significant tax burden, the company is not able to plan such investments, which in the long term are necessary to conduct profitable mining of copper ore and silver in the concession areas of KGHM in Poland. Reduction of the tax on the extraction of certain minerals by 15% - it is estimated to be 180 million in 2019 and 240 million in subsequent years - it will make additional KGHM funds available, which will significantly translate into long-term stability and development of the company. The growth trend of KGHM's debt, which could threaten the existence of many thousands of jobs in the long term, will be significantly slowed down. At the same time, it will be possible to plan the extraction and processing of ores for decades to come. KGHM will remain the main economic engine of the Lower Silesia region and the Copper Basin. Moreover, the funds released will allow increased investment, exploration and exploitation of copper ore deposits, which will translate into a better economic situation of the region, employees of the company and their families and local authorities. Revenue from other taxes and social expenditure from the KGHM Capital Group will increase, KGHM Foundation, as well as local governments, which are maintained, among others. from the danin public company. In the long term, the reduction in the tax on certain fossils can also translate into completely new domestic investments of KGHM outside the Copper Basin, located on acquired or acquired concessions, among others. Polyhalite deposits in the vicinity of Puck in the Pomorskie voivodship, copper deposits in the Lubuskie voivodship. This would allow more regions, not just Lower Silesia, to become significantly enriched by the economically efficient mining activity of KGHM Polska Miedź SA in their territory. The financial effect of reducing the tax on certain minerals by 15% will reduce the impact on the state budget by an estimated amount of PLN 180 million in 2019, as I mentioned, and 240 million in subsequent years. However, in the assessment of the applicants, these reductions in tax revenues from certain minerals will largely be offset by additional income from CIT and VAT paid by KGHM Polska Miedź SA and co-operatives, as well as indirectly through fiscal receipts of local government and PIT paid out by employees of the company. The bill does not affect the condition and competitive position of micro-entrepreneurs, small and medium-sized enterprises. The draft law does not lay down the rules for the taking up, pursuit or termination of business activities within the meaning of the Law of 6 March 2018. The draft law does not require an opinion, consultation or agreement with the competent authority and institutions of the European Union, including the European Central Bank, and is compatible with European Union law. It is proposed that the law enter into force on the day following the date of publication. The proposed provision does not affect the principles of democratic rule of law or the principles of legal certainty. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the applicants, I kindly request the support of the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u221	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I'd like to present a draft law on amending the tax law on the extraction of certain fossils. High Izbo! The current tax system in Poland inhibits investment activity of mining companies. A tax on the extraction of certain minerals was introduced in Poland on 18 April 2012. It represents the revenue of the state budget. It is important to discuss the meaning and validity of the tax on copper extraction in Poland, and to consider the possibility of introducing an alternative tax on fossil fuels or its elimination. An alternative fossil tax could be calculated on the basis of the market price of a tonne of copper extracted. When economic downturns occur, mining companies significantly reduce investment expenditure. We are facing a threat of unfulfilled investment projects in the mining sector during the economic period. The copper exchange rate is increasing and moving towards mid-2014 and 2018 results. The current quotations of this metal are USD 6492.75 per tonne as at 3 April 2019. Copper mining involves high initial capital expenditure. Increasing the capital stock assuming that extractive entities increase their commitment to work will increase productivity and thus increase the profitability of the extractive sector. In Poland there are documented copper reserves and at the same time there is relatively small extraction. National copper extraction and the world situation. The documented copper deposits in Poland amount to 28 million t - 8th place in the world. The most prosperous countries are Chile - 170 million t, Australia - 88 million t. The leader of copper mining is Chile, which gained 5.4 million t in 2018, followed by Peru - 2,4 million t. Mio t, China - 1.9 million t. Poland is 13th among producing countries at about 0.5 million tons. The investment cycle in the copper sector consists of four stages. The first is the recognition of the deposit, together with the construction of the infrastructure for its extraction, which is related to the second point, i.e. the development of the deposit, followed by the third point, i.e. the regular extraction of the product, up to the fourth point, i.e. the completion of the extraction with the liquidation of the mine. The tax on the extraction of certain fossils, for copper, in its present form, reflects the cost-effectiveness of the deposit, i.e. the rate of return on investment, which translates into reduced income from PIT and VAT. In conclusion, the proceeds from the fossil tax depend on the profitability of the entire project. The subject of taxation is copper extraction in the territory of the Republic of Poland or the territory of the exclusive economic zone. The tax shall not be subject to copper ore or processed copper ore ore which is not converted into copper ore by weight in a quantity not exceeding 1 t per month. The tax is paid by anyone who in the field of their business is mined by copper. The tax obligation already arises in the production of copper, i.e. on the day of production of the concentrate from the copper ore extracted by the taxpayer. The tax base is the amount of copper and silver contained in the produced concentrate or the amount of copper or silver contained in the ore of copper. Where the taxable person does not produce concentrate from the extracted copper ore, the taxable person, the producer, is required to measure the copper content of the extracted copper ore per day. With regard to copper extraction, the amount of tax for a given month shall be the sum of the product of the quantity of copper expressed in tonnes and the rate of tax. The taxpayer making copper extraction can calculate the loss and the way is described in the Act. Thus, the taxpayer determines the loss in the part concerning copper extraction in the proportion in which the revenue generated in the copper extraction tax year remains to the total amount of the taxable person's income determined on the basis of the income tax rules obtained in that tax year. The formula for calculating the copper fossil tax since 2012 is as follows. First version at the price of copper exceeding 15 thousand. PLN per tonne, mouth. 2.3,3% average copper price at 1,10% the average price of copper to the power of two and a half. In this case, the maximum tax rate may be 16 000. PLN per ton. The second version, when the average price of copper does not exceed 15 thousand. per tonne - average price of copper 12 thousand. multiplied by 44%, with a minimum tax rate equal to 0,5% the average price of copper. The proposed draft by Members of Law and Justice, Parliamentary Printing No 3312, implies a change in the formula for calculating the copper fossil tax actually consisting in setting the rate of the tax. Version one: the tax will be 15% lower. Second version: when the average price of copper does not exceed 15 thousand. zł per 1 t - average copper price is 13 012 zł - this is calculated according to the formula: tax rate times 0,374, with a minimum tax rate equal to 0,5% the average price of copper. Silver mining is also specifically taxed. In the field of copper and silver extraction, the tax obligation arises on the day on which the concentrate is produced from the copper ore extracted by the taxable person. The tax base for copper and silver extraction is the quantity of copper and silver contained in the produced concentrate. The tax base shall be the quantity of silver expressed in kilograms. Measurements of silver content shall be made each month on the basis of daily samples taken. Formula for calculating the silver fossil tax rate in the field of silver extraction. The amount of the tax for a given month shall be the sum of the products - first version. Second version: if the average price of silver does not exceed 1200 PLN per kg, the tax rate is the average price of silver, or 1000 PLN, times 0.75, with a minimum tax rate of 0,5 average price of silver. I'll remind you of the current one, i.e. as at 3 April 2019, silver listing on the stock exchange: 15,15 £1 for an ounce. The proposed draft by Members of Law and Justice, Parliamentary Printing No 3312, implies a change in the formula for calculating the silver fossil tax, which is in fact to determine the rate of the tax. Version one: the tax will be 15% lower. Second version: when the average price of silver does not exceed 1200 PLN per kg - the average price of silver is 1085 PLN - this is calculated according to the formula: tax rate times 0,638, with a minimum tax rate of 0,5% the average price. It is possible that the proposed amendments to the Mining Tax Act for some fossils will really translate into a lower tax calculated when extracting copper and silver. Therefore, this project should be submitted to the parliament of the Republic of Poland for further processing. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u251	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! I was honored to present the Parliamentary draft law from print no. 3288 on amending the Law of the Penal Code and some other laws. The purpose of the bill submitted is above all to tackle the problem that affects every country in the world, which is the problem of corruption. The aim we have set ourselves to achieve these objectives is to increase the transparency of public life by increasing public living standards and thereby increasing confidence in the institutions of the state. How are we going to do this? We want to implement this by amending the four laws already in force in the Polish legal order, starting with the Penal Code, through the law on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator, to the law on political parties and the law on public finances. With regard to the first of these laws, namely the Criminal Code Act, corruption-related offences, with public dishonesty, concern Article. 228.231. These laws prosecute and sanction crimes related to dishonesty in public life, while in our view they do not give sufficient guarantees to protect the State from persons who have committed gross mistrust towards it, to which the State has given an opportunity, and those who have not taken advantage of this opportunity. Therefore, we propose that those convicted of corruption offences, even those who will be subject to a mandatory ban on public functions, be deprived of public rights related to the question of choice. This is also linked to the issue of working in the whole public sector, in each of the three basic segments of power: legislative, executive and judicial, as well as in public authorities at local level, or to those who work in the private sector and who want to benefit from the possibility to participate in public tenders. The aim is to ensure that they are deprived of the possibility of concluding such agreements. We want this ban to be mandatory and lifelong. Since in the Polish legal order there is no doubt about the need to deprive a person who will cause a traffic accident under the influence of alcohol, for life a driver's license, since a person who committed a pedophilia crime has a lifetime ban on contacting children, we should also treat those who have committed corruption in public life. If we don't stigmatize this, this is the kind of situation that's gonna happen. In matters of minor importance, we also provide for mandatory application of these provisions. In this case, however, we envisage limiting the possibility of exercising public rights for a period of between 1 and 15 years. I'm finishing the criminal code. Please understand the intentions of the bill designers. Those who commit corruption crimes do not give the rightful guarantees to perform their functions in public life. They have failed you, and you should not be able to fail them again. I move to the next law that we want to change through the submitted project, namely the Act on the implementation of the mandate of Member and Senator. Ladies and gentlemen, as parliamentarians, Members and senators, in the current legal order, we can also be employed in commercial companies, including those whose owners or shareholders are the Treasury or local government units. The situation is, I'd say, double-curial. Firstly, one of the basic functions we perform as parliamentarians is the control function. We also control the public sector, i.e. assets belonging to the Treasury and local government units, including commercial companies owned by the State Treasury and local government. There may be some curial situations - a parliamentarian who controls himself can control himself because he is an employee of a State Treasury company. The second absurdity of this situation is the question of pathology, which can be compared to two things: the trampoline and the rotary door. During each term of office of the Sejm - the current term of office is no exception - we observe situations in which mandates: parliamentary or senatorial may be a trampoline to obtain very lucrative positions on boards of companies of the State Treasury. On the other hand, speaking of a rotating door, I mean when someone comes to the Sejm only to escape from it in a moment and take a very lucrative position in a company of the Treasury, where wages are often several times higher than the salary of a Member or Senator. We want to get rid of this pathology by means of a provision that prohibits the employment of parliamentarians, Members and Senators within 12 months following the resignation of a parliamentary or senator mandate. Another issue concerns the Law on political parties. Ladies and gentlemen, we want real disclosure of both expenditure and contracts or issues related to the maintenance of political parties, information on who supports them. Recently, we have heard that one of the newly formed political parties is supported by the Martians. I would very much like to know the name of an alien, a Polish citizen who supported this political party. We therefore demand that the quality of public life be adapted to the standards set out in our Constitution, so that issues concerning the financial flows of political parties, including those parties which receive millions of zlotys each year, are made public. We want an open register of influences on political parties' bank accounts. We also want an open register of expenditure. Citizens demand this because it is their taxes that the political parties receive subsidies. Of course not all, but the parties receiving subsidies raise the most doubts among the citizens, because it is from their taxes that most of you here in the Chamber as members of political parties receive the funds needed for your activity. A citizen has the right to know what a public penny is spent on. That is why we want two relevant registers to exist in the Public Information Bulletin: a register of payments containing in accordance with the Article. 24 Laws on political parties information who specifically contributed funds by name to the bank account of the political party and how much These are the means. We also want to know exactly what political parties spend their money on, what goods and services and whose wages. Ladies and gentlemen, the fourth and final element of the project concerns the Public Finance Act. Our Constitution in Art. 4 is much more precise. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 61 sets out the principle of transparency and transparency of public finances. Today, if a citizen wants to know what public funds are spent on, which are owned by the local government unit in which he lives, or by a specific public institution, he may use the provisions of the Public Information Act. The local government unit may also keep an appropriate register in the Public Information Bulletin. However, this is not a mandatory issue. We want to change that. We want to create a central register of contracts for all public institutions. We want two ministers to be involved in the creation of this register: the Minister of Digitisation and the Minister of Finance, who will define the standards of its structure by means of an appropriate common regulation. We want citizens to know what public funds are spent on - by means of civil law agreements, while maintaining standards concerning both the protection of personal data and the protection of commercial secrecy - but so that citizens know what public funds are spent on. Ladies and gentlemen, summing up, our bill assumes a basic objective: to increase the standard of public life in Poland. The easiest way to increase this standard is to eliminate dishonest people from public life, but above all, it is a matter of prevention, transparency of public life. And this bill guarantees it. In our draft law in art. 5 we predict that the bill will enter into force within 30 days. In this term of office of the Sejm, we will be able to learn the results of the operation of this law. I appeal to all political forces in the Polish Parliament who have the public good at heart to support this bill. Mr. Marshal, thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u255	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club, I would like to present my position on the draft law contained in Seym Print No. 3288. The proposal contains two groups of amendments: on the one hand, it concerns criminal sanctions for specific crimes, it concerns bribery and paid protection, and on the other hand it concerns transparency of public finances. Hence, at the outset, such a question as to whether it should not be two separate subjects of regulation. However, this is rather a point on the side, of a technical nature, to be clarified, but worth emphasising. As you yourself have pointed out in the explanatory memorandum of the bill, the current legal system contains quite strict penalties for the offences of bribery and paid protection, art. 228.231. Nevertheless, this is still a problem for our country, as well as for many other countries. This is a destructive phenomenon for both the administration and, of course, the economy, and it is therefore appropriate to look kindly at any legislative initiative to combat these phenomena. Therefore, it is certainly necessary to take a favourable look at the proposal to introduce this criminal measure, namely the revision of the article. 39 The Penal Code and the prohibition of office in state institutions, local authorities and all the institutions listed in this Article. However, there is one issue which, of course, raises doubts, namely the prohibition of applying for public contracts. Please note that if the provision remains in this wording, it will, of course, give rise to a number of reasonable doubts, namely: Will the person in question be able to act on his own behalf or on behalf of the management bodies or supervisory bodies in which he is to sit? Will she be able to be a procurator of the company that is present in such orders? Will this ban apply to public-private partnership initiatives, to which public procurement also applies? Thus, this wording will raise a number of questions and doubts. It is certainly critical to assess the proposal for amendments to the Article. 40 The Penal Code and, on the one hand, the introduction of a mandatory criminal measure and, on the other hand, the introduction of life-long restrictions laid down in that criminal measure. If we dedicate ourselves to reading the Penal Code, it will turn out that criminal measures that have been handed down for life are based on criminal offences committed in a redecisive way or in crimes committed against minors. If we were guided by this logic, in fact, in the event of another conviction for the same type of crime, the lifetime nature of the penalty would be much more justified. We are also not in favour of judging, ruling criminal measures in a rigid manner. We believe, however, that in this area it is always necessary to leave a certain degree of discretion to the courts because sometimes even very absurd situations can happen where the court is required to rule on a particular criminal measure, which can actually distort its essence. I would also like to look with some distance at the proposals for amendments to the Act on the implementation of the mandate of the Member and of the Senator and the introduction of a ban on taking up work in the context of the employment relationship 12 months after the expiry of the mandate. I understand your intentions, but please note that such a provision we can pour out a child with a bath, because it follows that within a period of 12 months a parliamentarians experienced, prepared in substance, who know the mechanisms of public administration may not even be able to occupy a normal position, e.g. For example, a referee, at the level of local government, with investment programmes, but after the end of these 12 months he will already be able to be president of a large company. So she is sure here, I have an impression, an inaccuracy, because such a record will not achieve the goal which led you, the authors of this project. I have similar doubts about public finance legislation. Namely, it is better to focus on the fate of money from subsidies, not specifically to the institutions of political parties, because we know from experience that the money from subsidies can be used not only by political parties. We hope that there it will be subject to the necessary changes.

The main causes of accidents which were the drivers of which in 2017 were primarily the failure to give priority to the journey, the lack of adjustment of speed to traffic conditions, incorrect behaviour towards pedestrians, the absence of safe distance between vehicles, incorrect overtaking. The main causes of accidents caused by pedestrians in 2017 are: careless access to the roadway directly in front of the driving car, crossing the road in an unauthorised place, access to the road from behind the vehicle, obstacles, entry to the roadway on a red light. Their scope has been extended to the basis of pre-school and primary education. For the first time, the content of communication education will also be communicated to pupils in secondary schools. Similarly, bikers are an important category, but also an increasingly common group of people moving on electronic scooters. The problem is particularly big cities. This group has not yet been sanctioned in any way. It is not known whether to treat such a person as a pedestrian or as a cyclist. This problem needs to be resolved quickly. The document presented by the Minister contains many charts, many sets of levels of dangerous events, which is understandable. The most important thing in my opinion is to identify solutions for the future, to identify actions that will improve safety. Unfortunately, there aren't enough of these. The level of road safety needs to be improved. This applies especially to local, municipal and district roads. There are a large number of pedestrian accidents on these roads. There are several reasons. The main and unchangeable over the years is bad visibility. Often people walking by the side of the road are invisible to approaching cars. Intensive information activities should be carried out, the basic principles of road traffic should be promoted in the information programmes. That's still not enough. I would add, by the way, that I consider the photographs of the Minister in the official document of the Council of Ministers to be a grave exaggeration.
