ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u392	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Health, I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the government draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and the law on medicinal activities, print No 3303. On 21 March 2019 the Marshal of the Sejm sent the above draft law to the Health Commission for first reading. The Health Commission held its first reading at its meeting on 3 April 2019 and examined the draft law. The report shall be published in the Official Journal of the European Union. The project was adopted by the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister of Justice. It was created in cooperation with the Minister of Health and the chief pharmaceutical inspector, i.e. institutions responsible for the supervision of the drug market, especially the safety of Polish patients whose right is access to products that save health, and sometimes and life. The project, which left the Health Commission, focuses on one very important aspect - it is a reduction in illegal drug exports, which thanks to the negotiations conducted by the Ministry of Health are often much cheaper than in neighbouring countries. Beneficial conditions for Polish patients attracted interested parties who, using the free movement of goods between Poland and the countries of the European Union, buy medicines in our country and then sell with high margins to wholesalers in Germany, Austria and Denmark. The fight against this has been the subject of many legislative initiatives by the Minister of Health. The activities so far focused mainly on extending the powers of pharmaceutical inspection and on enabling cooperation with the national tax authorities using a traffic monitoring system, which allowed inter alia. to effectively reduce illicit fuel trafficking. However, these actions did not include the introduction of criminal sanctions, which, in the government's view, are always the ultimate tool in the fight against irregularities. The introduction of these sanctions is the subject of a proposal submitted to the High House today. The scale of irregularities and the methods to which criminals resorted indicate that even exhaustion of the administrative route may not be an effective weapon in the fight against the drug mafia. Specially founded drugstores, wholesalers with double accounting, fake clinics, and even veterinary clinics - so far they were able to go those who at some moments in pharmacies lacked up to 200 medications. The amendment extends the circle of people who can be prosecuted for illegal drug exports and identifies cases where a pharmacy can sell the drug to another party. Criminal sanctions are also being tightened up. Today it is a fine, a limitation of freedom or up to 2 years in prison, while the project provides for up to 10 years of imprisonment for those who are involved in the export of drugs at risk from a list published by the Minister of Health. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed amendment closes the ongoing efforts to eliminate illegal drug exports for many years. It will allow even more intensive negotiations, thanks to which the Economic Commission acting at the Minister of Health lowers the prices of medicines that save the lives and health of Poles. Already today, some of them pay 100 PLN for the inhabitants of Słubice and his neighbour from behind the Oder 100 EUR. We want Polish patients to benefit fully from the fruits of these successes, and each pharmacy offers citizens a full range of products. Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! In view of the positive effects of the proposed amendments, I propose that the High Sejm adopt the attached draft law together with the amendments adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u393	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mrs Anna Czech, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u394	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! On behalf of the club Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club vis-à-vis the draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and certain other laws. The proposed amendment of the pharmaceutical law is primarily intended to eliminate the so-called "treaty." reverse distribution chain of scarce drugs by obtaining medicines from the retail market, e.g. from pharmacies, through pharmaceutical wholesalers, which will then resell these medicines abroad at a price which is several times higher than the permissible national price for medicines refunded. This activity causes a shortage of medicines on the Polish market, and consequently poses a serious risk to the health and even the lives of our patients. In this respect, the project envisages, firstly, the penalisation of certain behaviours of pharmacies, wholesalers and other entities to effectively counteract this turnover, export of medicines, foodstuffs for particular nutritional uses and medical devices, secondly, the penalisation of the behaviour of the wearers involved in this operation. The proposed provisions provide for criminal liability for the violation of the ban on drug disposal by the pharmacy, the breach of the ban on drug disposal by the wholesaler and the criminal liability for the operation of the fence involved in the reverse chain. The project also provides for the penalisation of exports and disposals of special purpose foodstuffs abroad to the Republic of Poland without the required notification, contrary to the opposition of the chief pharmaceutical inspector or before the deadline for notification. In addition, it provides for increased criminal liability for the above mentioned. behaviour in case of significant drug trafficking and illegal drug trafficking at risk of lack of availability. On the other hand, the project prohibits the purchase by wholesalers of drugs from pharmacies, creates a closed catalogue of entities to whom a pharmacy or a point may dispose of medicines, introduces a clear ban on the purchase by a medical operator of any medicines for a purpose other than the provision of benefits, introduces new powers for inspectors - it is a very important issue - to manage the opening of a controlled facility, premises, premises or storage facilities. It also provides for the possibility to inspect such an object or a clipboard. It introduces an obligation for the police or the Border Guard to provide the necessary assistance to inspection or inspection inspectors when they encounter resistance or when it is necessary to ensure that inspection activities are carried out without interruption. The solutions presented in the draft will ensure effective prevention of illegal export of drugs abroad, that is, the reverse chain of distribution of medicinal products. New regulations will increase the supervision of the pharmaceutical market and reduce illegal export of drugs abroad, and pharmacies will increase the resources of products at risk of lack of availability. On behalf of the club Law and Justice I am amending the draft law amending the Pharmaceutical Law Act. This amendment follows from the pharmaceutical community's report at the committee's meeting... This was discussed by Members of the Health Committee. Right now, Law and Justice are making such an amendment. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1 in point 6. in the following Article: 86a in the mouth. 1 add point 4 as follows: point 4 in order to supply entities other than those mentioned in points 1 and 2... as necessary to operators authorised to market medicinal products, medicinal products listed in the provisions issued pursuant to Article. Article 71 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 point 2. Justification: the amendment aims to enable purchases at a public pharmacy or at a pharmacy as a rule of the same medicines that can be bought at a public store. Operators other than patients and healthcare professionals will be able to purchase them. Necessary... operators authorised to market medicinal products, e.g. schools, kindergartens, workplaces to fill in the first aid kit. In summary, the Law and Justice Club is in favour of adopting the bill because it will prevent the lack of medicines necessary to save the health and life of our patients on the Polish market. Thank you very much. I'm making an amendment. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u420	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position towards the government's draft law amending the pharmaceutical law and the law on medicinal activities. The amendment partially implements into the Polish legal order EU Directive 2001/83/EC on the Community code relating to medicinal products for human use. The aim of the regulation is to strengthen the supervision and marketing of medicinal products and, as a result, to cut back the practice called the reverse chain of distribution of medicinal products. This is important because today the law does not limit the disposal of drugs by pharmacys in any way. The situations in which medicines to which you pay, medicines to be refunded, are carried out abroad and sold at a profit are extremely high. The scale of this activity is so large that it is said that the drug mafia is active in Poland, and the wholesalers operating in this industry are practically impunity. Everything, of course, because of the lack of data of preparations on the Polish market, takes place at the expense of Polish patients and their health and life. The regulations contained in the project are specific when the pharmacy will be able to dispose of medicines. In this way, the acceptable direction of distribution of medicines is comprehensively regulated, and thus the patch is broken today by the law. A number of new provisions are also envisaged in the novel to provide pharmaceutical inspectors with full access to all warehouse premises, including closed ones, and to allow them to use inspections to assist police officers or Border Guards. I am convinced that these solutions will contribute to the improvement of the control activities carried out and will eliminate situations in which PIF employees are intimidated in the course of their work or are restricted to moving freely around the facility they are checking. I have no doubt that in order to meet the objectives of this project, it is also important to clarify the voivodeship's powers to impose financial penalties for the illegal sale of medicinal products, medical devices and foodstuffs for particular nutritional purposes by medical operators and new, much more severe sanctions, which leave no doubt that anyone who, contrary to the law acquires, disposes, imports, exports or stores a medicinal product will be subject to a penalty. I trust that the regulations discussed today are designed to protect Polish patients and will effectively counter illegal drug exports abroad. About Free and Solidarni supports this project.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u460	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I submit a report to the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution on commemorating the 150th anniversary of the birth of Józef Mehoffer. The Commission, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 2 April 2019, invites the High Sejm to adopt the draft as follows: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating the memory of Józef Mehoffer on the 150th anniversary of his birth. On March 19, 2019 the 150th anniversary of the birth of Józef Mehoffer - one of the most outstanding representatives of the art of Young Poland, painter, graphic artist, cartoonist, designer of stained glass and interiors, stage designer and educator. The artist was born in a socialized Austrian family. He studied painting at the Krakow School of Fine Arts and at the Vienna and Paris Academys. He was a friend of Stanisław Wyspiański, with whom during his studies he performed polychromy under the direction of Jan Matejka in the church of Mariacki. In Mehoffer's works, stained glass windows and wall polychromes take a prominent place and are among the greatest achievements of decorative art in Poland. The artist is the author of vault decorations and polychromes and stained glass in Wawel Cathedral. His works are decorations in the Cathedral of St. Jana in Lviv, in the cathedral in Włocławek and in the church in Turku. The international fame was given to Józef Mehoffer first place in the competition for the design of stained glass windows designed to decorate the Gothic collegiate of St. Santa in Fryburg. One of the most famous canvases in his legacy, i.e. the mysterious symbolism of 'Weird garden', has won many awards in Poland and abroad. Józef Mehoffer was a professor of the Academy of Fine Arts in Krakow and twice served as the rector of this university. He co-founded the Society of Polish Artists. He was one of the artistic associations of Vienna, Paris and Brussels. For their creative activities Józef Mehoffer received many awards, including in 1937 he was awarded the Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland 'for outstanding merits in the field of Polish art and propaganda abroad'. In 1986 the artist's house on Krupnicza Street in Krakow was opened to the public as a branch of the Kraków National Museum. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland honors the memory of Józef Mehoffer and praises the artist's creative achievements. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u466	This year marks the 150th anniversary of the birth of the outstanding Polish artist Józef Mehoffer, one of the most outstanding representatives of the art of Young Poland. He was a student of Jan Matejka and a peer of Stanisław Wyspiański. We know him as a painter, graphic artist, artist, but also a designer of stained glass, furniture, interiors, fabrics or theatre scenery. In his work he used various artistic techniques, thus gaining rich, deep expression of artistic expression. Józef Mehoffer worked on artistic graphics, applied acidorite, aquatint, performed autolithography and utility graphics. He decorated the covers of the books by designing eclipses, initials, ornaments, vignettes, and bordiurs. That's why he got the title of Art Nouveau Decoration Master. The artist also designed trademarks, banknotes, stock sheets and even posters. He was also a portraitist who paid particular attention to the dress of his models. The collection of works by the Polish painter contains nine thematic cycles devoted to ballet. In his work in the area of sacral art he dealt with the polychromy of the Wawel vault and the Armenian Cathedral in Lviv. An important work of the artist is stained glass and polychrome in the Church of the Sacred Heart of Jesus in Turk. Józef Mehoffer also designed the windows of the Latin Cathedral in Lviv, the Church of Our Lady of Comfort in Żyrardów or the Cathedral of St. Santa Claus in Fryburg, Switzerland. The period of the Young Poland is a time of great symbolism, in which the artist created his greatest or most famous painting works: "Pegaz imprisoned," "Portrait of his wife against the background of the pegaza," "Dream Beauty," "Dreamy Garden," "May Sun," "Red Umbrella." The 20th interwar anniversary is a change of color and painting technique and other important works, including landscapes. For their creative activities Józef Mehoffer received various distinctions, among others. The Commander's Cross of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland (1923), the Golden Wawrzyn Academic Awarded by the Polish Academy of Literature (1935), as well as the Commander's Cross with the Star of the Order of Rebirth of Poland (1937). Józef Mehoffer was a professor at the Academy of Fine Arts in Cracow and twice held the dignity of the rector of this university. He co-founded the Polish Artists' Society. The beginning of World War II meant for Mehoffer to stay in a camp in Czechoslovakia, where he was deported from Lviv. Due to the diplomatic intervention of the Vatican State and the Italian Government, he was able to return home. During the occupation at Mehoffer's house, music meetings were held, in which they participated, among others. Zygmunt Digat or Egon Petri. Józef Mehoffer died on July 8, 1946 in Wadowice. Today in the Polish Seym we have the opportunity to celebrate the memory of a great artist whose works delight with the timbre, the brightness of the sun and the delicateness of the drawing.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u469	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft resolution on commemorating the 150th anniversary of Stanisław Wojciechowski's birth. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Paragraph 37 of the Act. 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft resolution to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading on 27 March 2019. The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 2 April 2019, requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution. Let me read it. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on commemorating Stanisław Wojciechowski on the 150th anniversary of his birth. On March 15, 1869, Stanisław Wojciechowski was born in Kalisz - an outstanding co-worker, scholar and statesman, as well as the second President of the Republic of Poland. He started his independence activity in the Polish Youth Union 'Zet'. He continued it in the Polish Socialist Party, where with Józef Piłsudski, among others. he edited the magazine "Worker" and on emigration in the Foreign Union of Polish Socialists. He was an active activist of the cooperative movement, editor of the weekly 'Społem', as well as organizer of the first convention of organizing the cooperative movement of consumers. After Poland regained its independence, from January 16, 1919 to June 9, 1920 he was Minister of Internal Affairs in the Governments of Ignacy Paderewski and Leopold Skulski. In 1921 he joined the Polish People's Party 'Piast'. After the tragic death of President Gabriel Narutowicz, the National Assembly elected Stanisław Wojciechowski as President of the Republic of Poland on 20 December 1922. During his duties as President, thanks to exceptional features of character, he eased tensions and disputes and sought compromises, putting the state's interests ahead of ad hoc party interests. In general opinion, he was an extremely righteous and honest man. During the May coup in 1926, Stanisław Wojciechowski stood by the government and then decided to resign from the President's office. After his withdrawal from political life, he devoted himself to cooperative activities. He was also a professor of the General School of Rural and Higher School of Commerce and headed the Cooperative Research Institute. During that time he wrote many significant books and publications on cooperative topics. He spent the period of the Second World War in Warsaw. He died in 1953 and was buried in a family tomb at the Powęzkowski Cemetery. Stanisław Wojciechowski deserves Poland well. On behalf of the committee, I ask the High Sejm to adopt this resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u471	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the parliamentary draft resolution on commemorating Stanisław Wojciechowski on the 150th anniversary of his birth. Stanisław Wojciechowski coat of arms Nałęcz was born in 1869. He was the son of the January insurgent. During his studies he began his independence activity in the anti-Russian conspiracy. He was involved in the work of the Polish Youth Association Zet and later of the Polish Socialist Party, where he and Józef Piłsudski edited the newspaper "Worker." He was also a courier and emissary. Several times imprisoned by the Russians, he was active in emigration in the Foreign Union of Polish Socialists. He was expelled from France as a particularly dangerous political activist. In 1905, when the anti-Independence faction of the young people was given the advantage in PPS, he withdrew from political work. He then became involved in cooperative activities, among others. editing the magazine "Company." He particularly focused on food cooperatives. During World War I he worked in the Central Civic Committee and later in the National Committee of Poland. He advocated cooperation with Western countries and Russia, until the Bolshevik coup, and reconciliation between national democrats and socialists. After regaining his independence, he was Minister of Home Affairs from 1919 to 1920. He took up, among others. decision to delegate the Communist Workers' Party of Poland as Soviet agents. He then started lectures on cooperativeness at the University of Commerce. He was active in the Polish People's Party and without success ran for president of the Republic of Poland, elected by the National Assembly, although he was favored by Marshal Józef Piłsudski. After the assassination of President Gabriel Narutowicz, he was re-registered as a candidate for President of the Republic of Poland. He was elected, this time in the first round, to this honorable office. During his presidency, Stanisław Wojciechowski tried in particular to support Władysław Grabski's government and its reforms. At the same time, it shaped the constitutional custom by introducing a constructive vote of distrust - entrusting the formation of a new government to the leader of the largest group which disproves the previous government. He also accepted, contrary to the March Constitution, the custom that the Sejm sits permanently, not in the session system. He never closed a session of the Sejm in spite of such a constitutional order. On 12 May 1926, in the face of the military demonstration, Marshal Józef Piłsudski met him on the bridge of Prince Poniatowski and declared, according to him, the defence of the honour of the Polish Army, the faithfulness of the constitution and the defence of the government of Vincent Witos. The marshal was hoping for cooperation and agreement with the president, but that didn't happen. On 14 May, in view of the progress of the troops of Piłsudski's faithful, he resigned from the office, not wanting a dangerous civil war for Poland's independence. In the following years he was again involved in cooperative activities and lectures at Warsaw universities. During World War II, when the Germans arrested his son for defending Jewish lawyers and offered Wojciechowski his release in exchange for signing a statement about the alleged illegalness of the Polish government in exile, he strongly refused and his son died shortly in the German camp in Auschwitz. After the war Wojciechowski refused any collaboration with the communist invader. He died in 1953. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Stanisław Wojciechowski was a man of great integrity and integrity, always putting the good and independence of Poland above all else. The Law and Justice Club will vote for a resolution to pay tribute to President Wojciechowski. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

I also intervened with the Marshal of the Voivodship and there are no visible actions of the Marshal. It's time to take care of the mincing business, the mincings are waiting for it. It is time to talk in the European Union about measures for entrepreneurs to modernise their workplaces, to invest in clean air, and to ensure that national programmes include resources when negotiating the EU Clean Air Regulation so that they can be allocated to the decommissioning of cinderellas and to negotiate funds for local authorities to build an integrated heating network in the so-called. urban functional areas. Now is the time to talk in Brussels, in the European Commission and in the European Parliament. I run for elections to the European Parliament to take care of the most important things for us: clean air in Mielec, Rzeszów, Przemyśl, in every place in Poland, so that we do not be the ungrateful leaders of the ranking of countries with the highest air pollution. Mielczeń, but also Poles and Poles need to know that they will breathe fresh air. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u483	Please take the floor of Mr Piotr Müller, Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u484	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I regret to inform you that Jan Didak, the leading Polish fighter, Olympic medalist and multi-time Polish boxing champion, died on 27 March at the age of only 51. In 1988, at the Seoul Olympics, he won a bronze medal in semi-medium weight. He also competed in the European Championships in Gothenburg in 1991, where he won a bronze medal, and in the 1991 Sydney World Championships. In the Polish Championships he reached three times for the title of Polish Champion: in 1987, in 1990 and in 1991, while in 1988 he was Deputy Country Champion. At the turn of the 80s and 90s he was one of the greatest talents of Polish boxing, beside Andrzej Golota and Dariusz Michalczewski. Between 1988 and 1991 the four-time winner of the boxing classification for the best Polish fighter, the two-time winner of the Golden Gloves. In the colours of Black Słupsk the gold medalist of the Polish team championships in 1990 and eight times representative of the country in interstate matches. According to official statistics he scored as many as 171 fights, 159 wins, 2 draws and only 10 loses. He ended his career in 1991 because of a serious hand injury. He was a player of GKS Hawks in 1982 and 1986 and Black Slupsk in 1987 and 1991. In 2005, he coached the Blacks' boxing section. Although he wanted to be a football player, fate directed him to other sports tracks. Born in Czeladź, he was forced to move to the Hawk during martial law. Jan's cousins trained boxing, the future fighter also decided to try, borrowed gloves and went to practice. He loved boxing from the start. The first duel ended with defeat. However, he accepted further challenges from which he came out victorious. He became one of the best technically prepared players in Poland. The first Polish championship won at the age of only 19 years, and after the bronze medal of the Olympics in the intermediate category reached just a year later. He almost didn't go to Seoul at all. There was a long-standing joint injury, but he decided to hide this information from the trainers because he knew he wouldn't have been allowed to start. Despite his injury, he defeated other opponents for points, and he won the medal by fighting with practically one hand. In 1991, during the fourth pilgrimage of the Holy Father John Paul II on behalf of Polish athletes, he welcomed the Holy Father. He continued the way of great sports successes, and subsequent victories confirmed experts and fans in the belief that we have a great talent of the world class in Poland. Bronze at the European Championship in Gothenburg confirmed the form of a fistfighter. The next Olympic Games were to take place next year. Jan Dydak was one of the favorites, but he decided to end his career just because of a hand injury. After the gloves were suspended, he had to go to Germany. After returning fortunately, he had the opportunity to train the young people of Black Slupsk. Coach Kazimierz Adach, also Olympic medalist, and club colleagues Dariusz Michalczewski, Jacek Gminski and Cezary Banasiak remember him as a great athlete and an even bigger friend. He was very involved in promoting the Olympic idea, visiting schools, attending meetings with children and young people. He actively participated in youth classes and training, supporting new talents. For the rest of his life he was involved in cooperation with the Polish Olympic Committee. Unfortunately, he's been struggling with cancer for a long time. 27 March Słupsk was grieved, the city said goodbye to an outstanding athlete and a great, good man. He left his wife Teresa, the children of Magda, Dorota and Sebastian, and the grandchildren of John and Jędrzej. In the memory of loved ones will remain warm, conflictless, optimistic man, always ready to help others. High Izbo! I would like to inform you that I have applied to President Andrzej Duda for the posthumous award of Jan Dydak as the Order of the Rebirth of Poland for outstanding merits for sport. Mr. John, rest in peace. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u485	Thank you. Please take the floor of Mr Jack Wilk, the Confederation.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-04-03-sejm-79-1.u486	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! There is a rather strange electoral campaign in progress for the Europarliament, in which there is hardly really any mention of the Europarlament, there is no talk of the European Union, there are issues related precisely to the Brussels Government, but for example. various bizarre programs are proposed, mainly distributions, mainly socialist ones, which are, of course, on credit and are completely detached from reality and have only an effect in the form of buying voters. Therefore, I believe that Poland deserves a real program, a development program, a program that can push Poland forward, a program that can actually change our reality. That is why I have decided to allow myself to propose my own five points, which are indeed a real pro-development programme. The first point is the support of the state, the state administration in a large part or the essential part of state bureaucracy on blockchain technologies. Thanks to these technologies and opportunities, bureaucracy, the number of documents, papers, etc. can be radically reduced. Estonia's example, which introduced this solution, shows that it is possible to save up to 2% on such a system gross domestic product. Under Polish conditions it would be about PLN 40 billion per year. Secondly, the second point - conversion of the 500+ program into a classic free educational voucher. This is particularly important at the moment when teachers protests are underway, when everyone is wondering how to fix Polish education. There is no other way to fix this school than to introduce healthy competition between schools. A classic free education voucher causes that anyone who gets a voucher like this, i.e. anyone who has a child, can choose any school, with any program, with any owner. And under such conditions, everyone can afford a good private school, and thus can compete in the educational services market and raise the level of education, which unfortunately is increasingly catastrophic due to the fact that we still have post-peerel order, post-communist order in our schools, e.g. appearing, for example, in keeping a post-communist teacher's card. It is then about introducing a single tax instead of all current state taxes. This tax is VAT and excise duty, because unfortunately we must have this because of our presence in the European Union. It is easy to calculate that if we eliminate all taxes and leave only VAT, then at the appropriate rate of effective revenue to the budget will be identical, and they will actually be higher, because this is how we eliminate the grey zone. Of course, we are talking about maximum simplified VAT with one rate on everything and with many other simplifications. Point 4 - abolition of the payment of contributions to the Social Security Office and the NFZ. Poland's pension system is one big disaster and absurdity in every way and there is no reason to pretend that this system can be maintained, this system simply needs to be destroyed and ultimately built completely different. However, for now, at least in the first step, Poles who know very well that the ZUS will not have any pensions, allow them to simply retire themselves, endure, as in Germany, at least from entrepreneurs, the forced payment of pension contributions, are to leave the same to citizens who are able to take care of themselves well enough and can predict how to retire without help, without the grace of the ZUS, which robs us in a criminal way. And the last point - the introduction of a civil veto, like Switzerland. This is a special referendum in which citizens can reject any law adopted by the Sejm by means of a qualified number. This is a very good solution, because it blocks the extremely harmful and stupid solutions that populist governments adopt, and it is easy to get rid of the law that harms everyone. And so I suggested the so-called. Five Wolf, which is a real reform program that can actually make real changes and improve citizens' lives, not some idiotic programs that are just supposed to rob us and transfer money from one pocket to another, especially before the elections. As the great thinker Alexis de Tocqueville said, democracy will end when governments realize that they can buy citizens with their own money, what is happening right now. Thank you very much.
